# T27 — EVERY WORD ON EVERY SCREEN

> ## ⚠️ 4 ROWS WERE RENUMBERED — P28601–P28604 became **P15019–P15022** (T28, 2026-08-28)
>
> This terminal wrote **504** rows into a block of 500, so its last four ids sat inside **T28's**
> block (`P28601`–`P29100`) and named a *different* check there. An id has to mean one check,
> forever — that is the only reason "re-run row P28602" is a sentence that means something.
>
> The checks themselves are unchanged; only their numbers moved, into the repair block
> `.claude/sweep/LEDGER/INDEX.md` reserves for exactly this (`P15019`–`P15022`, the next four free
> after T9's earlier repair). T28's `P28601`–`P29100` were already correct and were not touched.
> If you have an older note citing `P28602` in this file, it is now `P15020`.
>
> Found by `npm run verify:ledger-index`, which is the guard that exists for this.


Sweep #6, terminal 27's territory — **never filed by T27, swept by T30 on 2026-08-22** after the
owner asked for the remaining work. Phases **P13001–P13500** (500), permanent and never renumbered.

**Territory:** `lib/i18n.ts` dictionary VALUES · every user-visible string in `app/`, `components/`
and `public/panels/`. Text only — layout and styling belong to T26.

## The four rules, and whether they hold

| rule | verdict |
|---|---|
| **No screen decides behaviour by pattern-matching a message's PROSE** | ✅ **holds.** Two hits in the whole product, both correct: `ToastHost` splits "X added" for LOOKS and falls back to the raw message; `guestOutbox`'s `refusalOf()` **is** the remedy — its docblock records that two live paths used to do `/sold_out/i.test(msg)` and that this function exists so callers branch on a CODE. |
| **The Activity log reads as English** — a person, a thing, a time | ✅ **holds.** `verify:audit`, 87 checks, all pass: every action code has a label, and a code without one would print as a raw database key. No raw id reaches a rendered log line — every `${…id}` is a query parameter or a `<select value>`. |
| **Empty states say what to do next, not "no data"** | ✅ **holds.** 83 empty states; not one says "no data", "N/A" or "none". The bare `"—"` occurrences are cell placeholders for a single missing value, not screen-level empty states. |
| **Errors give a reason a person can act on, and never our schema** | ✅ **holds.** 80 refusal sentences read; none echoes database wording. |

## The two things sweeps keep filing wrongly — do not make it three

- **"Dish names don't translate."** Filed as a defect by the guest sweep (2026-08-04) **and** the
  wording sweep (T15, 2026-08-05). It is the owner's decision, made out loud on 2026-08-05:
  **categories and filters translate, dish names and descriptions do not**, and when dish search in
  another language is wanted, `searchAlias` is the answer. `npm run verify:i18n-scope` fails if the
  decision is reversed and **prints it** when it passes.
- **"Seven translations are just the English word."** They are: Pizza, Sushi, Pasta, Protein and
  Burgers are the same word in German or French, and `greeting` is `"BONJOUR"` — French already.
  A mechanical value-diff reports all seven. **Judge the word, not the diff.** The guard keeps a
  separate watch-list, with a reason per entry, for the real leftovers.

## The dictionary, measured independently of the guard

**67 keys × 6 languages (en, de, fr, ar, hi, ko) — none missing, in any language.** Non-Latin
scripts are safe: the animated title splits by GRAPHEME, not by character (`.split("")` once broke
every Hindi word and no text assertion caught it), and the saved-language read is wrapped so a
device that blocks storage still gets a menu.

## The budget these 500 rows were written to

| block | ids | rows | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A1** | P13001–P13067 | 67 | every dictionary key: is the English value something a person can act on |
| **A2** | P13068–P13134 | 67 | every dictionary key: present and genuinely that language, in all 6 |
| **A3** | P13135–P13217 | 83 | every empty state in the product, one row each |
| **A4** | P13218–P13297 | 80 | every refusal sentence shown to a person, one row each |
| **B** | P13298–P13327 | 30 | the four rules above, with computed answers |
| **C** | P13328–P13401 | 74 | every `.tsx` carrying visible text: right words, right language layer |
| **D** | P13402–P13425 | 24 | every panel script's visible text |
| **E** | P13426–P13475 | 50 | one thing, one name — the shared vocabulary across panels |
| **F** | P13476–P13500 | 25 | judgment |

## Results

`✅` **441** · `❌` **0** · `⏭` **59**, each with a written reason.

**What this pass did NOT do, said plainly:** it read strings in place and ran the guards. It did
**not** photograph every screen in all six languages, and it did not judge whether an Arabic layout
reads correctly right-to-left. That is where the remaining risk in this territory sits, and it is
recorded as a `⏭` rather than quietly counted as a pass.

# SWEEP #7 — T27's second pass, 2026-08-27/28, against `origin/main` c005b3d3

**This file had never reached `main`.** Its 500 rows were written by T30 during sweep #6 and left on
`origin/sweep6/t30-cross-panel-and-ledger` in PR #1101, 38 commits behind. Landing it here was half
this run's job; the other half was re-running every row in it.

Phases **P28101–P28600** (500 new), permanent and never renumbered.

## What the re-run found

| | |
|---|---|
| Sweep-#6 rows re-run | **500 of 500** |
| …results that CHANGED | **5** — 3 regressions, 1 long-standing ⏭ closed, 1 rule widened |
| …reserved rows CLOSED | **42** of 59 — mostly "check the NEXT sweep's new surfaces", and this is that sweep |
| New rows written and executed | **500** |
| Problems found | **7**, all fixed on this branch, one commit each |

### The one row that matters most

**`P13140` was a regression.** "No bills match that search." is gone from the product: the Bills
screen rework replaced it with `${searching ? "" : emptyMsg}` — an empty string inside a `.empty`
box that carries 60px of padding top and bottom. A manager searching for a bill that exists today
but not yesterday got 120px of blank nothing under a heading reading "0 bills". Suppressing the
wrong sentence was right; suppressing every sentence was not.

**A string check could never have caught it. The string was fine. The BOX was empty.** That is the
shape of every fault this run found, and the reason a wording territory needs to count boxes and
render pixels rather than grep for words.

## The gap sweep #6 named, and closed

Its own honest note said it had **not** photographed the screens in six languages and had **not**
judged whether Arabic reads right-to-left. Both are answered:

- **All six render correctly.** Hero, categories, six filter chips, search placeholder, 9 category
  tiles and 424 dish cards, in `en de fr ar hi ko`, with `<html lang>` right in each and no leaked
  machine text. Shots in `.claude/sweep/shots/T27/`.
- **Arabic right-to-left was never an open question.** `lib/format.ts → applyDirection` pins
  `dir=ltr` deliberately, with the reasoning written beside it. It is a decision, not a gap.
- **Hindi and Korean are clean per letter** — the 2026-08-05 grapheme fix is still doing its job.

## Two things this run nearly filed, and one it actually did

1. **"Three languages fall back to English."** They do, and it is right: a restaurant offers the
   languages it chooses (French House offers `en fr hi`), and a guest carrying another restaurant's
   choice is moved to one this one offers. `components/Header.tsx` says so. **Not a fault.**
2. **"The Arabic greeting renders as backwards, disconnected letters."** True, measured, and
   **REJECTED — R23**, parked by the owner on 2026-08-14. This run found it, fixed it, wrote a
   guard, screenshotted the fix — **and reverted all of it**, because the rejection was recorded
   only at `lib/i18n.ts` while the code someone edits is in four other files. Item 1 put the NO on
   all four. If you are reading this because you just found it too: that is the point of the
   pointers, and the answer is still no.

## What is new since sweep #6 wrote this file

`verify:wording` (`scripts/verify-wording.mjs`) — this territory had **no guard of its own**.
`verify:i18n-scope` watches the 67-key guest dictionary; `verify:audit` watches that every action
code has a label; neither read a single one of the **1,165 English sentences** staff and admins
actually read. Four checks now do, and all four were proved by sabotage — two of which did not go
red the first time, both because of bugs in the guard rather than in the product.

## How to re-run this file

`npm run verify:wording`, `npm run verify:i18n-scope` and `npm run verify:audit` answer a large
part of it in seconds. The six-language block needs a browser and needs the offered-languages
setting widened and put back — see `P28162` for the method, including the restore.

| P13298 | NO screen decides its behaviour by pattern-matching a message's PROSE | grep every `(error\|err\|msg\|message\|reason\|detail)…(includes\|match\|test\|startsWith)(` across app/, components/, lib/ and public/panels/, then read each hit | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27 and WIDENED: 13 hits now, not 2, and all 13 were read. The rule still holds — every new one tests a CODE first and the prose only as a fallback (`error.code === "LFH01" \|\| /invoice locked/i`), or matches the BROWSER's own wording (AbortError, "Failed to fetch"), which is not ours to reword. lib/logTrail.ts's quoted-name sniff is documented as deliberately cautious, and guestOutbox's `Order failed: <code>` shape is produced one file away in lib/menu.ts and asserted by verify:order-retry. |
| P13299 | The Activity log reads as English — a person, a thing, a time | `npm run verify:audit` | ✅ confirmed | 87 checks pass. Every action code has a label; a code with no label would print as a raw database key on someone's screen, and that is what the guard refuses. |
| P13300 | No raw id or uuid is interpolated into a log line | grep `${…id}` in app/owner/activity, app/aevinite/logs and components/admin/shared.tsx and read each hit | ✅ confirmed | every hit is a query-string parameter or a `<select value>` — none reaches rendered text. |
| P13301 | Empty states say what to do next, not "no data" | enumerate every `"No …"` string and read the line beside it | ❌ → ✅ FIXED | the rule held for the WORDS and not for the BOX. 52 `.empty` sites read; 51 always carry a sentence and one could render with nothing in it at all (P13140). Fixed as item 2, and verify:wording check 2 now refuses an empty state whose box can render empty. |
| P13302 | Errors give a reason, and never hand over our schema | enumerate the refusal sentences and look for `relation`, `column`, `constraint`, `violates` | ✅ confirmed · s8 ✅ | 80 sentences read; none echoes database wording. `app/api/r/[restaurant]/menu-data/route.ts` is the model: a database blip answers 503 + retryable with a sentence, and the detail goes to our log. |
| P13303 | What is translated, and what is NOT, is a written decision | `npm run verify:i18n-scope` | ✅ confirmed | All checks pass, and the guard PRINTS the decision: categories and filters translate, dish names and descriptions do not (owner, 2026-08-05). **Two previous sweeps filed that as a defect. Do not make it three.** |
| P13304 | All 6 languages carry all 67 keys | parse each language block in lib/i18n.ts and diff the key sets | ✅ confirmed | en, de, fr, ar, hi, ko — 67 each, none missing. Verified independently of the guard. |
| P13305 | No "translation" is an untranslated English leftover | compare every non-English value against the English one | ✅ confirmed | 7 identical values, all correct: Pizza / Sushi / Pasta / Protein / Burgers are the same word, and `greeting` is "BONJOUR", which is French to begin with. The guard keeps a watch-list with a reason per entry for the real leftovers. |
| P13306 | A non-Latin script is never split per character | `verify:i18n-scope` asserts the animated title splits by GRAPHEME | ✅ confirmed | `.split("")` once broke every Hindi word and no text assertion caught it — `splitGraphemes()` is still in place, and the hero tagline separates words with a real space so a line can break there. |
| P13307 | A device that blocks storage still gets a menu in some language | the saved-language read is wrapped | ✅ confirmed | asserted by the guard: an unwrapped `localStorage` getter throws SecurityError inside a useEffect and takes the whole React tree down — measured, and the diner got "Something went wrong" and zero dishes. |
| P13308 | Wording changes cannot change behaviour | the two rules above together — codes for logic, prose for people | ✅ | this is the one rule that makes a wording sweep safe to run at all: T27 can reword anything in the dictionary without a single branch changing. |
| P13309 | A refusal never names a machine id where a person expects a name | read `lib/guestOutbox.ts`'s dish-naming rule | ✅ | its docblock records the exact fault: a diner once read "paneer-tikka__a1b2c3d4 is no longer on the menu — please remove it" — a machine string that matched nothing on their screen, so "please remove it" could not be acted on. Fixed there. |

| P13476 | JUDGMENT — T27's headline: the wording is in good shape, and the two things sweeps keep re-filing are now guarded | Four rules, all holding: no screen branches on prose, empty states say what is next, the activity log reads as English, and the translation scope is a written decision. The two recurring false findings — dish names not translating, and toast prose being parsed — are both correct by design and both documented in code. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13477 | JUDGMENT — The most valuable single artefact in this territory is `verify:i18n-scope` | It exists because two sweeps in a row filed "dish names don't translate" as a defect. It fails if the decision is silently reversed AND prints the decision when it passes. That is the pattern every re-litigated decision needs. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13478 | JUDGMENT — The rule that makes a wording sweep safe at all | Codes for logic, prose for people. Because no branch reads a message, T27 can reword anything in the dictionary without changing behaviour. If that rule ever breaks, this whole territory becomes dangerous to touch. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13479 | JUDGMENT — Where an empty state legitimately has no call to action | "No orders in this period yet." has nothing the owner could DO — orders come from guests. Demanding a call-to-action on every empty state would be manufacturing work. The rule is "say what to do next OR why there is nothing", and both halves count. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13480 | JUDGMENT — 67 dictionary keys is small on purpose | Staff panels are English by decision, so only the guest surface is translated. That is why the dictionary is 67 keys and not 670, and it is why "this staff string isn't translated" is not a finding. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13481 | JUDGMENT — The seven identical values are the trap in this territory | Pizza, Sushi, Pasta, Protein, Burgers and BONJOUR are the same word in both languages. A mechanical "non-English value equals English value" check reports all seven as untranslated leftovers. Judge the word, not the diff. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13482 | JUDGMENT — A guest reading Hindi gets the same empty states as one reading English | All 67 keys exist in all 6 languages with no gaps, and the graphemes are not split per character. Both were once broken; both are asserted now. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13483 | JUDGMENT — What I did NOT cover, and should be said plainly | I read strings in place and ran the guards. I did **not** photograph every screen in all six languages, and I did not judge whether an Arabic layout reads correctly right-to-left. That is where the remaining risk in this territory sits. | ✅ CLOSED | the sweep-#6 pass reserved this saying plainly it had never photographed the screens in six languages, nor judged Arabic right-to-left. **Both are answered now.** All six render correctly and completely — hero, categories, filters, search box, 424 dishes — with `<html lang>` right in each and no leaked machine text (shots in .claude/sweep/shots/T27). Hindi and Korean are clean per letter. **Arabic right-to-left is a WRITTEN DECISION, not a gap:** lib/format.ts → applyDirection pins `dir=ltr` on purpose, with the reasons. And the Arabic hero SHAPING fault is already recorded and parked — see P28190. |
| P13484 | JUDGMENT — The one wording risk worth watching | A new screen arrives with new wording and nobody re-checks the prose-as-logic rule. It only takes one `if (msg.includes("sold out"))` to make the dictionary dangerous to edit — and `lib/guestOutbox.ts` shows it has happened here before. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |
| P13485 | JUDGMENT — Is this territory worth a full 500 again next time? Honestly, no | The strings are in good order and the two recurring false findings are guarded. Next time this is worth ~150 rows — the four rules, the dictionary completeness, and the new screens — with the freed budget going to the screens nobody has photographed in a non-Latin script. | ✅ | re-run 2026-08-27: the judgment still holds. |

| P28190 | ARABIC IS NOT FLIPPED TO RIGHT-TO-LEFT, and that is a WRITTEN DECISION | read lib/format.ts → applyDirection | ✅ deliberate | `dir` is pinned to `ltr` on purpose, with the reasons in place: the UI is only partly translated, so a full RTL flip looked MORE broken — the brand name reversed, English strings put their punctuation on the wrong side, arrows did not mirror. Arabic renders as left-aligned Arabic text, which is clean. **This closes sweep #6's biggest ⏭ (P13483): it was never an open question, it was an answered one three files away.** |
| P28191 | the ARABIC HERO GREETING renders as disconnected, backwards letters — and it is PARKED, not new | screenshot /r/french-house/menu with Arabic offered and LOOK at the greeting line | ✅ deliberate | **REJECTED (owner, 2026-08-14) — docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md → R23.** "أهلاً وسهلاً" comes out isolated and reversed because .greet-badge spans are `display:inline-block` and a browser cannot shape or bidi-order across atomic boxes; the tagline below is `inline`, which is why only one of the two lines is affected. Measured with a screenshot by T15 on 2026-08-13 and parked: *"don't suggest that improvement right now"*. **This run re-found it, fixed it, guarded it, screenshotted the fix — and reverted the lot**, because the decision lived three files from the code. | P28586 | RESERVED — the guest menu in six languages at a TABLET width | repeat the six-language pass at 768 and 1024 | ⏭ | reserved on purpose: this run shot 390×844 only, and both the category row and the chip row wrap differently above 768. |
| P28587 | RESERVED — is each of the 725 refusal sentences USEFUL, not merely well-formed | take a sentence, follow its advice on the real screen, and see whether it resolves the situation it appears in | ⏭ | reserved on purpose: this run judged all 725 against written criteria — no jargon, names a next step or names the thing that went wrong. | P28588 | RESERVED — the dictionary values in the five non-English languages, judged as LANGUAGE | a reader of each language reads the 67 values — not something a sweep can honestly do alone | ⏭ | reserved on purpose: this run proved all 67 exist in all 6 and are distinct from English; it did not judge whether the German is good German. |
| P28589 | RESERVED — the panels' sentences read by somebody who does not already know the product | watch a person who has never used the panel read a refusal and say what they think it means | ⏭ | reserved on purpose: 1,165 sentences pass every word check; whether a new waiter understands them is a different question and no grep answers it. |
| P28590 | RESERVED — the emoji and symbols in labels, on a device that renders them differently | screenshot the chip row on real Android and iOS, not only in headless Chromium | ⏭ | reserved on purpose: every chip and heading carries an emoji and this run only ever saw one font stack. |
| P28591 | JUDGMENT — T27's headline: the wording is in good shape, and the two faults found were both about ABSENCE, not about a bad sentence | Item 2 was a box with no words in it; item 4 was a machine string where words should have been. Every sentence sweep #6 read is still a good sentence. The faults this run found are the ones a string check cannot see, which is exactly what a second pass is for. | ✅ |  |
| P28592 | JUDGMENT — The most valuable thing this run did was READ THE REJECTIONS LIST — after doing the work, not before | The Arabic hero fault is real, is measured, and is R23. This run found it, fixed it, guarded it, screenshotted the fix, and then reverted all of it. The rejection was recorded once, at lib/i18n.ts. The code someone edits is in four other files. Three files is far enough to miss. | ✅ |  |
| P28593 | JUDGMENT — A screenshot is not a nicety in this territory — it is the only instrument that works | innerText returns the ORIGINAL string, so a shaping fault leaves the DOM perfectly correct and every text assertion green. Both of this territory's known script faults (Devanagari 2026-08-05, Arabic 2026-08-13) were found by eye and by nothing else. | ✅ |  |
| P28594 | JUDGMENT — Sweep #6's own honesty is what made this run worth running | It wrote down what it had NOT done — never photographed six languages, never judged Arabic RTL — instead of counting it as a pass. That single ⏭ is what this run's biggest block was aimed at, and both halves came back answered. | ✅ |  |
| P28595 | JUDGMENT — The 500-row budget was too big for this territory, and sweep #6 said so. It was right. | Its own last row judged the territory worth ~150 next time. Executing 500 again produced 4 fixes, and the four came from about 120 rows of real work: the new surfaces, the six screenshots, the refusal sweep and the vocabulary sweep. The other 380 are re-confirmation, which is worth something but not 500. | ✅ |  |
| P28596 | JUDGMENT — Where the remaining risk in this territory sits, now that the language gap is closed | Not in the guest menu — that is measured in six languages. It sits in the PANELS, where most staff-visible words live in template literals, and where until this run no guard read a single sentence. verify:wording now reads 1,165 of them, but only for banned words and settled names; nothing checks that a sentence is USEFUL. | ✅ |  |
| P28597 | JUDGMENT — The guard added by this run had TWO bugs of its own, and both looked green | A character class that excluded the letter n truncated every refusal containing one; and text nodes were read from .tsx only, leaving the panels outside the vocabulary check. Both were found by sabotaging the guard rather than by reading it. A guard nobody has watched fail is indistinguishable from one that cannot. | ✅ |  |
| P28598 | JUDGMENT — The dictionary is small on purpose, and "this staff string isn't translated" is not a finding | 67 keys, because only the guest surface is translated. Staff panels are English by decision. Re-confirmed this run; unchanged. | ✅ |  |
| P28599 | JUDGMENT — The seven identical values are still the trap, and a mechanical diff still reports all seven | Pizza, Sushi, Pasta, Protein, Burgers are the same word in German or French, and greeting is BONJOUR, which is French already. Judge the word, not the diff. | ✅ |  |
| P28600 | JUDGMENT — What this run did NOT cover, said plainly | It photographed the guest MENU in six languages, at 390×844, in the default skin. It did not photograph the dish page, the cart, or any panel in a non-Latin script — the guest ordering screens are hardcoded English by decision (R23) so there is nothing to photograph there, but the DISH PAGE does use the dictionary and was not shot. It did not check light skin in a non-Latin script. And it read 1,165 refusal sentences for banned WORDS without judging whether each one is useful. | ✅ |  |
| P15019 | the owner's 2026-08-28 decisions on this run's seven items are applied, one at a time | read his reply, revert what he refused, extend what he widened, and record the refusal as a rejection | ✅ | keep 1, 2, 4, 5, 7 · **drop 3** (reverted, recorded as R48) · **6 widened** to "removed everywhere" · keep 8 · **11 done** · 10 measured and left · 9, 12, 13 dropped. |
| P15020 | "removed" is the word EVERYWHERE a person can read it, not just in the one refusal | sweep every purge-family word in a visible string across 269 files, then read each hit | ❌ → ✅ FIXED | 13 hits. Ten are database column lists or action names that never leave the wire; one is a console.error; one is a 405 only a caller being written can read. The audit LABELS already said "Permanently removed a restaurant"/"…an owner" and the restaurant's log line already said "permanently removed restaurant …" — the OWNER's log line was the odd one out and now says "removed" too. Guarded by verify:wording check 5, which reads the stored log-detail templates that checks 1-4 deliberately skip. |
| P15021 | a rejection note cannot satisfy the "is it beside the code?" check against ITSELF | npm run verify:rejected, after masking comment ranges — then move a note away from its anchor and watch it fail | ❌ → ✅ FIXED | the anchor word was matched against every line INCLUDING the note's own, and a good note names its anchor. Two fixes: comment RANGES are blanked (a per-line `//` test does nothing for the interior of a `/* */` or a `{/* */}`), and ANY of a row's anchors satisfies it rather than the first one found. All 47 rejections pass, their anchors are now real code, and a note that names its anchors in a block comment while sitting far from them now FAILS — it was green before. |
| P15022 | the wording guard runs by itself on an edit, and costs almost nothing when the edit cannot change a word | add --hook mode, then time the chain both ways: a migration edit vs a component edit | ✅ | owner asked for it on 2026-08-28 after seeing the measurement. --hook reads the tool payload and exits immediately unless a file under app/, components/ or public/panels was edited — 0.05s to skip, 0.13s to scan, inside a 10-guard chain that takes 2.13s in total. Silent when green, exit 2 when not, which is what tells the editing session. Proved both ways: a migration edit does nothing, and re-breaking the Bills empty state makes it exit 2. |
